# src/CLAUDE.md — Change Log

> **Created:** July 31, 2026
> **Purpose:** The `**Last Updated:**` header chain and the `VERSION HISTORY` table
> formerly carried in `src/CLAUDE.md`. Both record the same landings from different
> angles: the chain is prose, the table is versioned rows.
> Split out of `src/CLAUDE.md` on July 31, 2026. Content is **verbatim** — moved, never edited or reordered.

**Appending:** add a new `**Last Updated:**` entry at the top of the chain and
re-label the previous newest `**Last Updated (prior):**`; add a matching row at the
top of the VERSION HISTORY table. Do not edit historical entries.

## Header chain

> **Last Updated:** August 6, 2026, latest same day (v2.78 — **AR passes 3–5 over the #29/#41 T2
> landing — 1 High, 4 Medium, 7 Low, all fixed.** The High is a coverage defect of the exact class this
> project keeps paying for: **`SeasonLoop`'s career-wired match boot had never executed anywhere.** Every
> test wiring a career ran `QuickSimAll`, and every engine-mode test in the assembly built the loop
> *without* a career — so the sole production call site of #29's entry-fatigue seam, and the only place
> the ERR-030-009 availability filter meets a real `MatchEngine`, shipped unrun. Fixed by extracting
> `SeasonLoop.BootFixtureEngine` (internal — the `ShouldPlayThroughEngine` precedent, extracted for the
> same reason: inline, the branch is reachable only by playing a full 90-minute match, which no suite here
> does) plus three `EnginePath_*` cases. **M1:** the entry-fatigue unit tests could not distinguish
> `entryFatigue[local]` from `entryFatigue[k]` — `CoherentSquad` lays positions out in slot order, so
> local and slot coincided at every probed index and the whole file passed under the bug; the new case
> puts the only goalkeeper at the last local. **M2:** `PlayerCareerStates.FromBlocks` now **copies** the
> two state arrays — it borrowed them, and the documented restore path feeds it the very arrays
> `SeasonSaveManager.Load` returns in `SeasonSaveContents`, so every holder of those contents was a second
> writer of #29/#41 state. v1.1 closed that on the save route and left it open on the only other route in.
> **M3:** `ScheduleFor` goes internal; the public focus surface is `PlayerCareerStates.TrySetFocus`, a
> command that resolves fresh per call. v1.1's `RosterGeneration` made a cached handle's staleness
> *detectable by a caller who remembered to check* — the documented-not-enforced standard this codebase
> rejects everywhere else. **M4 (new, ERR-030-026):** where the ROUND sits among the KD-2 day-slots was
> settled by `AdvanceToNextFixtureDay`'s loop condition and written down nowhere. The emergent order is
> play-then-process-matchday: right for #41's occurrence draw, wrong for the recovery countdown sharing
> its atomic step, so **every injury runs one matchday longer than its tier**. Adopted, documented at all
> three determining sites, and locked — the balance pass now fits recovery tiers against a stated
> convention. **L:** `SeasonSaveManager`'s version rows were 1.7-then-1.6 with two `Modified:` headers;
> `SeasonLoop` v1.7 claimed a "public World accessor" that landed internal; both block types' docs
> disclaimed the decode ordering `FromBlocks` depends on; `CareerTestRoster.Build`'s summary described the
> wrong index; the quick-sim's silence about match-entry fatigue is now argued; the `Save` overload no
> longer recommends a cross-thread `ActiveMatch` read; `Blocks_RoundTripThroughBothCodecs` verified one
> codec despite its name and held for two sets of identical fresh states. No schema, format, RNG or
> behaviour change. **NO GATE RUN** — no .NET SDK, installer still 403. Prior entry below.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 6, 2026, later same day (v2.74 — **AR pass over the #29/#41 T1 landing — 2
> High, 2 Medium, 3 Low, all fixed.** The headline defect exists *because* ERR-029-004 succeeded:
> pinning #29's byte layout to match #41's made the two blocks byte-for-byte the same shape, and every
> sub-blob format in the save stack sits at version 1, so a version gate separates one *generation* of a
> format from the next and never one format from another. Each codec therefore decoded the other's bytes
> cleanly, completely and silently — severity tiers as training focuses, recovery counters as
> conditioning cursors — verified by executing a byte-exact model of both formats in both directions.
> Fixed in two layers (`ERR-029-005` / `ERR-041-009`): a leading `*_SAVE_MAGIC` per block, checked
> before the version, and the typed `TrainingBlock` / `MedicalBlock` replacing two of
> `SeasonSaveCodec.Encode`'s five consecutive `byte[]` so the transposition that triggers it is a build
> error. Second High: `SeasonSaveManager.Save`'s `trainingClubs`/`medicalClubs` defaulted to
> null-meaning-empty, which at T2 would let an omitting call site silently drop a season of conditioning
> and injury history; both are now required. Mediums: `TrainingSaveCodec.Encode` gained the encode-side
> value gates its sibling already had, and the four framing helpers duplicated across both codecs moved
> to `SaveBlobFramingHelpers` in `deterministic-sim`. **Still no gate run** — no .NET SDK, installer
> still 403 at the proxy. See VERSION HISTORY 2.74.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 6, 2026 (v2.73 — **#29 / #41 T1: the two save codecs and their composition
> into #30's season save.** New `src/training-system/TrainingSaveCodec.cs` + `ClubTrainingStates.cs` and
> `src/injuries-medical/MedicalSaveCodec.cs` + `ClubInjuryStates.cs`; the season frame gains both blocks
> and `SEASON_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` goes **2 → 3**. `season-save.asmdef` gains references to both
> assemblies (the one-way `#30 → #29/#41` composition #29 §4.1 / #41 §4.1 specify), and
> `training-system.asmdef` gains `DeterministicSim` for `CanonicalSerializer` — a reference §4.1 already
> declared but T0 had no use for.
>
> **The container types earn their place.** `ClubTrainingStates` / `ClubInjuryStates` bind a club id and
> its parallel id/state arrays once, at construction, for the reason `TrainingSchedule.TrySetFocus`
> exists: every club in a generated league has the same squad size, so a codec API taking ids and states
> as separate arguments accepts one club's ids beside another club's states without tripping a length
> check, and then persists the wrong club's players silently.
>
> **Canonical order.** Encode sorts to ascending `(ClubId, PlayerId)` — on a copy, so a live roster array
> is never reordered under whatever else holds it — and decode requires strictly ascending keys. Equal
> state sets therefore produce equal bytes regardless of caller order, `encode ∘ decode` is a fixed
> point, and a duplicate key fails loud at encode instead of reaching a file with no defined winner.
>
> **What is deliberately NOT validated on decode:** `Condition`, `TrainingFatigue` and
> `RecoveryRemaining` against their `[GT]` ceilings. Enforcing a tunable bound at load means a designer
> lowering `ConditionMax` in config turns every existing save into an unloadable file — a tuning edit
> causing data loss. Only structurally impossible values are refused (negative accumulators and counters,
> undefined focus/severity ordinals) plus #41's F1 coherence rule, which runs on **encode as well as
> decode** so the codec cannot write a file no load of it accepts.
>
> ERR-029-004 and ERR-041-008 filed and resolved, spec + code same commit; see `spec-error-log.md` v1.64.
> **NO GATE RUN** — still no .NET SDK in the authoring environment, installer still 403 at the proxy. The
> csproj generator runs clean (64 projects) and meta integrity passes; CI on push is the gate.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 5, 2026, later same day (v2.68 — **AR pass 5 over #29/#41: 2 Medium, 3 Low.
> The headline is a number nobody had measured — the daily injury probability the wired system would
> actually produce.** Every occurrence test in #41's suite forces the outcome with
> `CertainOccurrenceRisk() = InjuryRiskMax × 4`, a value the producer chain **cannot reach** (#29 clamps
> at the ceiling, then #41 subtracts its own mitigation), and the one test that drives the real producer
> asserted orderings and bounds only. So the suite proved an injury happens when forced and none happens
> when disabled, and never established that the wired system produces any at a sane rate — the
> ERR-030-014 shape exactly. Driven end to end at today's `[GT]`s: a **freshly inserted regen is 23%
> likely to be injured on day 0** (`ConditionStart` is 3000 below `ConditionMax` and
> `LowConditionRiskWeight` carries that shortfall at weight 1 on the same 10000 the draw denominator
> derives from, so "not yet at peak" is priced like "half dead"); **half-fatigued is 43% per day**; and
> the **DEFAULT Balanced focus converges on exactly 0, permanently** — its daily load equals
> `FatigueDailyRecovery`, so fatigue never accrues and the shortfall term reaches zero in ~39 days. A
> subsystem whose default path emits zero events is indistinguishable from one switched off. **Not
> retuned** — both assemblies are inert and KD-W1 forbids landing a `[GT]` governing an unwired
> subsystem — so the three numbers are locked by a characterization test that makes the balance pass
> leave a visible diff, and the one lever that rescales all of them is named: the shared `InjuryRiskMax`
> ceiling the `[CROSS]` mirror turned into a single dial. M-2: `T-TR-NEU-001`'s assertion is itself
> vacuous — `TrainingInput` is an EMPTY struct, so `AreEqual(Neutral, anything)` cannot fail — and the
> v1.2 comment named that root cause only for FR-TR-006, leaving a reader to believe KD-8
> behaviour-neutrality was locked. It is the claim this whole T0 landing rests on. Lows:
> `DrawOccurrence` now refuses a non-positive denominator (zero threw on its own; a **negative** ceiling
> did not — the `ulong` cast made the modulo a no-op and produced a signed garbage draw that still
> classified); `AssignRecoveryDays` refuses the `None` tier, whose 0 days the F1 floor would have raised
> to 1 against `Severity == None`, inverting the invariant the floor exists to protect; and a production
> doc cited a test METHOD NAME in another assembly, restaging the staleness v2.67 had just cleared.
> **Still no gate run.**)

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 4, 2026 (v2.56 — **Tilted-view revision — KD-P4a-2 (owner call).** P4a first shipped a flat top-down view with ball height faked by a sprite lift and a capped size ramp. The owner reversed it to an FM-style view — from above, **tilted back from vertical, slightly off centre** — since the ball only needs to be visible on and above the pitch, not scaled. The revision **deletes more than it adds**: with a tilted camera height is a real world axis, so `BallHeightViewOffsetPerMetre`, `BallHeightScalePerMetre` and `BallMaxHeightScale` are gone, along with `BallRenderModel.SpritePosition`/`SpriteRadius` and `MatchRenderProjection.HeightScale` — and with them the AR pass's M-5 finding and its 10 m saturation limitation, which stop existing rather than needing a retune. New: `PitchCameraRig`/`PitchCameraPose` (height, tilt-from-vertical, lateral offset — a placement is a decision, so it is gate-compiled, and the pose is two world points because `Quaternion` is not in the shim) and `PitchViewProjection.ToWorld`/`ToWorldGround`/`TryGroundHit`. **The one real cost is the click inverse:** screen position is no longer affine in pitch position, so `TryGroundHit` does a ray/ground-plane intersection; `Camera` is not in the shim, so the Unity side supplies the ray and the math stays gate-tested. Survivors, each for a reason — the **shadow** (under any tilt a lofted ball separates from the pitch point it is over, the one cue perspective cannot supply), the corner→centre re-origin (it is the ground plane), and `FollowBallCamera` (it decides *where* the camera looks). Two things recorded rather than left implicit: the engine's Y becomes the world's **Z** and its Z the world's **Y** — an axis swap, the same trap class as the corner origin, locked by its own test (seven tests fail if it is inverted) — and `FollowBallCamera`'s pitch clamp is now **approximate**, since it describes a rectangle of visible ground where a tilted view sees a trapezoid; kept deliberately, as its job is keeping the target near the pitch rather than exact framing. **Full dotnet gate: PASSED, 0 failures** (whole tree green; match-client-core 112 → 129, match-engine 368 unchanged — no sim source was touched). The entry was first written while the run was still in flight and recorded as *not yet reported*; this line replaces that provisional wording with the run's actual result.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 3, 2026, latest same day (v2.54 — **Interactive Unity client P4a — the
> host-free render model.** P4 is split into **P4a (every render decision, gate-compiled) and P4b (the
> binding)**, which turns the standing "keep logic out of `MonoBehaviour`s" rule from a discipline into
> a phase boundary. The ordering argument is the one that put P6's head-less scenario ahead of P4, one
> level down: land the decisions where the gate can see them, and the host is left verifying binding.
> **New production** (`match-client-core/`): `PitchViewProjection.cs` v1.0 — the single documented
> coordinate adapter §7 requires, mapping engine **corner-origin** metres (Ball Physics #1 §1.2) to a
> **centre-origin** view plane at 1 unit per metre, with the inverse a pointer click needs and an
> on-pitch predicate. Centring is not cosmetic: it makes a home-end position and its away-end mirror
> differ only in sign, which is what reduces the mirrored assertions this repo's #8 ERR-008-002 history
> demands to one line each. `PitchMarkingKind.cs` / `PitchMarking.cs` / `PitchMarkings.cs` v1.0 — the
> IFAB marking catalogue as shapes, read from the **existing** `MatchViewerConstants` `[FIXED]` values
> rather than restated, with both ends emitted from one loop over a sign so a marking cannot be right at
> one end and wrong at the other; `MARKING_COUNT` is written as a formula over `TEAM_COUNT` so the count
> and the emit loop cannot disagree. The centre-circle D-arc and the corner arcs are **deliberately
> absent** — neither has a `[FIXED]` constant and the browser viewer draws neither, so adding them would
> mean inventing geometry here and diverging the two Views. `MatchRoster.cs` v1.0 — match-**constant**
> per-slot data (team id, slot-ordinal shirt number), moving the shirt-numbering rule out of the browser
> viewer's inline JavaScript into gate-tested C#. `AgentRenderModel.cs` / `BallRenderModel.cs` /
> `MatchRenderProjection.cs` v1.0 — the resolved draw states: positions from the P3 interpolator's
> buffer (that is what is actually being drawn), every discrete cue from the newest captured frame (cues
> do not interpolate — blending a card into existence over 16 ms is meaningless), the possession ring
> (whose radius doubles as the `HasBall` predicate so the two cannot disagree), and the ball's shadow /
> height-lift / capped-scale cues. Allocation-free, fail-loud on every shape mismatch. **Colour-free by
> design** — a palette has no correct answer a test could assert, and `UnityEngine.Color` is not in the
> shim's surface, so `TeamId` is the renderer's palette key and everything upstream of it is here.
>
> **The finding — KD-P4a-1, a stale cache that predates this pass.** `LiveMatchStreamer` cached team ids
> *and* goalkeeper flags at construction under the comment "roster metadata never changes across a
> match". True of team ids — a bench player belongs to the team whose bench they sit on — and **false of
> goalkeeper flags**: `MatchEngine.SubstitutePlayer` copies the bench player's flag into the on-pitch
> slot, so a keeper substituted for an outfield player (or the reverse) moves which slot is the
> goalkeeper and the cache silently disagrees with the engine from that tick on. It had been drawing the
> keeper ring on the wrong player in the browser viewer since P1, and a Unity roster type built on the
> same accessor would have inherited it wholesale. **Fix:** `LiveAgentCue.cs` v1.1 gains `IsGoalkeeper`
> — the first cue added through the extension mechanism KD-P1-6 created the struct for — sampled every
> tick beside the cards and bench slot it rides with (`LiveMatchStreamer.cs` v1.6); `MatchRoster` holds
> **no** goalkeeper flag at all, so the stale copy cannot be reintroduced; the streamer's
> `IsGoalkeeper(int)` accessor is kept and re-documented as boot-time only (a caller needs roster
> metadata before the first frame exists); and `LiveMatchServer.cs` v1.3 reads the frame cue when there
> is a frame, fixing the harness with **no JSON key and no viewer-script change**. Re-reading the engine
> from the accessor was rejected — that is exactly the off-sim-thread tear-read the streamer's
> single-writer invariant exists to prevent, and it is why the flag was cached in the first place.
>
> The cue constructor gains a fourth parameter with **no default**: a producer that forgets it must fail
> to compile, not silently report that nobody on the pitch is a goalkeeper. Its two-ints-two-bools shape
> is guarded by a field-mapping test rather than collapsed into carriers, and the four existing
> construction sites are updated. **Modified:** `MatchClientConstants.cs` v1.2 (the render-cue `[GT]`
> sizes v1.0 deferred "to P3/P4 with their consumers" — marker and possession-ring radii, ball radius,
> the three ball-height cues, and the marking spot radius). **Tests:** four new fixtures in
> `match-client-core/tests/` and three new cases in `match-viewer/tests/LiveMatchFrameCueTests.cs` v1.2
> — including the substitution test run for **both** teams, and the
> `PositionsComeFromTheInterpolatedBuffer_NotTheFrame` lock, which is the non-vacuous predicate here:
> without it the projection would pass every other assertion while quietly ignoring P3 and stepping
> motion at the sim rate. **No `SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_VERSION` change, no new RNG stream / domain tag / draw
> site, no draw-order change, no engine-behaviour change** — the new cue is sampled from an existing
> read-only accessor. **Full dotnet gate: PASSED, 0 failures** (whole tree green; all 30 suites reported) — `match-client-core` 65 → 103, `match-viewer` 39 → 41, `ui-framework` 50 (unchanged), `match-engine` 368 passed / 8 skipped (unchanged; no `match-engine` source is touched by this landing), every other suite unchanged.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 3, 2026 (v2.51 — **Conversion at contact (§5.Z.23) — ERR-011-008.** The

> **Last Updated (prior):** August 3, 2026, later same day (v2.52 — **Interactive Unity client P6 — the
> head-less closed-loop scenario.** The §5-P6 acceptance scenario, landed ahead of the P4 render skin
> for the reason §12 gives: `match-client-unity` sits in `generate_projects.py`'s
> `SHIM_EXCLUDED_ASMDEFS`, so every P4/P5 line is invisible to `tools/dotnet-ci`, while this is
> gate-checked on every push. **New production:** `match-client-core/TickStampedCommandReplay.cs` v1.0 —
> the log-driven replay cursor. It enqueues each entry immediately before the tick whose pre-tick
> `CurrentTick` equals its `AppliedTick`, which is exactly where the original drain read the clock, so
> a replayed run re-stamps identically and the log is a fixed point of its own replay. `FromTick(log,
> n)` slices the resume tail. An out-of-order log and an entry whose application point has already
> passed are both refused fail-loud — silently skipping either produces a run that is NOT the log's run
> while still reporting success, which is the precise failure this class exists to rule out.
> **Modified:** `MatchSession.cs` v1.2 — constructor split into a static `BootEngine` (boot-only
> mutators) plus a private engine-agnostic wiring ctor, so the new `RestoreFrom(blob, squads)` builds a
> whole session over a RESTORED engine without re-applying boot mutators (`ConfigureSquads` throws on a
> ticked engine; re-staging tactics would overwrite restored state). Adds `TickOnce()` — the head-less
> deterministic advance, which drives the REAL `LiveMatchStreamer.TickOnce()` seam rather than a
> parallel client-side tick path, so the hook, the frame capture and the auto-pause rule behave in the
> scenario exactly as they do under paced playback; it refuses fail-loud once `Start()` has been called,
> because two threads ticking one engine is a data race and a comment is not a guard. Adds
> `CurrentTick` / `MatchEnded` / `CurrentSnapshotDigest` reads and `CaptureSave()`, the durable capture
> that rides the `ServiceOnce` seam (§6.3) — **§6.3's drained-empty-before-capture invariant is now held
> by ORDERING** (one sim-thread pass: drain and apply, then capture) rather than asserted, and an
> `Encode` fault is latched and rethrown to the `CaptureSave` caller instead of escaping the pre-tick
> hook and killing the background pacing thread (the same isolation posture `MatchClientDriver` takes
> for a refused command). The save handshake uses `Interlocked`/`Volatile` rather than a lock held
> across `ServiceOnce`, which would have established the opposite lock order against the streamer's tick
> gate. `match-viewer/AssemblyInfo.cs` v1.1 — `InternalsVisibleTo("TacticalDirector.MatchClientCore")`;
> the seam stays internal to `match-viewer`, so nothing is widened for the browser viewer.
> `tests/match-client-core-tests.asmdef` gains `TacticalDirector.TestingStrategy`. **Tests:** the two
> §5-P6 scenarios in `tests/MatchClientClosedLoopScenarios.cs` (+ `…ScenarioTests.cs`), owning specs
> {16,19,21}, paths under `SCENARIO_PATH_CROSS_SPEC_PREFIX`, in the composition root's own test assembly
> per the `MatchEngineCapstoneScenarios` precedent — `match-client-command-log-replay` (script → log,
> log → digest-identical replay, log is a fixed point) and `match-client-save-restore-replay`
> (save@90 → restore → replay the post-90 log to tick 180 == the uninterrupted run). **The load-bearing
> predicate is the control run:** a third session with the same `MatchSetup` and NO commands must
> diverge, in a bounded window around the first command. Without it every other predicate passes on a
> command channel that does nothing — the exact shape of the capstone that asserted a match ticked while
> every match was a 0–0 deadlock (ERR-030-014). A ten-command script drives all three live mutators
> across BOTH teams on both sides of the save tick; a home-only script would repeat the #8 ERR-008-002
> asymmetry mistake in the client layer. Also +`tests/TickStampedCommandReplayTests.cs` (10) and six new
> `MatchSessionTests`. **One predicate was deliberately NOT written:** a "queue is drained at capture"
> check inside the scenario would be true regardless of capture order there, so the §6.3 invariant is
> locked instead by a unit test that enqueues a command immediately before `CaptureSave` and requires it
> back applied and logged. No `SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_VERSION` change, no new RNG stream / domain tag / draw
> site, no draw-order change — the client observes and drives pre-existing public mutators only.
> **Gate: NOT RUNNABLE in this environment** — the network policy blocks the .NET SDK download
> (`curl` to `dot.net` returns a 403 from the agent proxy), the same constraint this supplement's own
> header records. Verified instead by exhaustive manual review against source, plus a
> `generate_projects.py` run confirming the new `TestingStrategy` reference resolves and the test
> project is generated. The gate runs in CI on the PR.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** July 27, 2026 (v2.44 — **Goalkeeper save pipeline — the §5.Z.15 lever, measured and
> discharged; three correctness defects fixed, and the goal rate barely moved.** §5.Z.15 recorded the next
> lever on the engine's goal rate as *"the quality of the goalkeeper's save"*. That framing has a premise
> inside it — that saves happen and are merely poor. **They did not happen:** measured over three full
> 90-minute matches with a new instrument, the keepers made **zero** hand contacts with the ball across
> all six keeper-matches. No instrument in this tree had ever reported a goalkeeper statistic of any kind,
> which is why nothing noticed. New `src/match-engine/tests/GkSaveDiagnosticTests.cs` (env-gated
> `TD_GK_DIAGNOSTIC=1`, assertion-free per the ERR-030-014 convention) reports the pipeline as a **funnel**
> — `armed → SAVE committed → Anticipate → Diving → Airborne → contact → caught` — because a funnel
> localises WHERE a chain breaks rather than only reporting its end empty; every stage up to and including
> the dive fired healthily (14–41 commits, 13–31 dives a match) and the chain ended at contact, at exactly
> zero. It reads through the new `MatchEngine.TestOnly_GoalkeeperState`, which uses the SAME public
> `CaptureState` the v19 snapshot writer uses. Three defects, each independently sufficient:
> **ERR-011-003** — the dive had no direction (`ComputeDiveDirectionLateral`'s only non-zero branch is
> gated on `SaveIntent.DeflectionTarget`, which the sole producer sets `null`; measured mean
> `|diveDirectionLateral|` = **0.000** across every dive ever launched, closest approach **2.75 m short**
> over a whole match — the conflation is the cause, since `DeflectionTarget` is where the keeper wants to
> PUT the ball, not where it should DIVE; now the linear XY interception of where the ball will cross the
> keeper's plane, bounded by a new `[GT] DivePredictionHorizonS`). **ERR-011-004** — a catch was
> **arithmetically impossible**: `OnShotExecutedEvent` had zero callers in production *or tests*, so
> `reactionWindowAchieved` was permanently 0 and §3.5.1's `quality = 0.70·rawHandling + 0.30·reaction`
> capped quality at a **measured 0.630** for a PERFECT keeper against `CatchThreshold` 0.78; new
> `MatchEngine.NotifyKeeperOfShot` fires on the shot's CONTACT frame (§3.2.1 dates perception from the
> strike), and the method now takes the attributes because it is frequently the first call of an episode,
> earlier than `CommitSaveIntent` — the only other writer of that snapshot (KD-P4). **ERR-011-002** — the
> keeper woke for the wrong end of the pitch and never stood down: the orchestrator computed the third the
> keeper's own team ATTACKS and passed it to a parameter documented as the OPPOSING team's, and
> `Anticipate` had no exit but a dive, so keepers held it **76–92% of every match**; fixed per §5.Z.12
> (*"a pair has two places that must agree; a mirror has one"*) as one signed distance to the keeper's own
> goal with both predicates renamed from the KEEPER's perspective, plus an `Anticipate → Set` exit.
> **Measured effect:** dive direction 0.000 → **1.000**, best miss 2.75 m → **−0.07 m**, contacts
> **0 → 15**, Anticipate share 76–92% → **11–18%** — and **goals per match 15.3 → 15.3, UNCHANGED**, against
> football's ~2.7. **That last number is the result:** three genuine defects, each of which had to be
> fixed before a save was possible at all, are worth about one goal a match. §5.Z.15's lever was real and
> is now spent; it was not where the mass is — the same shape as §5.Z.9 and §5.Z.11, where the measurement
> refuted its own brief. New acceptance scenario `match-engine-goalkeeper-saves` (#19 ScenarioRunner,
> Tier B, 4 seeds × 15 min, 56 s) asserts **reachability** stage by stage and deliberately pins **no** save
> percentage and **no** goal rate; **11 of its 12 predicates fail on the pre-fix engine** — verified by

> **No `SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_VERSION` change, no new RNG stream / domain tag / draw site, and no change to the
> draw order** (the fixes alter the arguments to existing draws, never their number or sequence).

> predicate (ball parked at x = 75 precisely because that woke a team-0 keeper pre-fix), re-anchored to
> x = 30 with its intent preserved — the Phase-H *"tests encoded the old contract"* class.
> **Recorded, NOT fixed — and now the honest next lever, each verified against source:** a shot
> essentially **cannot miss** (aim hardcoded to 0.732 m inside the post; `ShotExecutor` never reads
> `finalDirection.z`, so the whole vertical half of the placement and error model is inert); there is
> **no crossbar** (`BallCollision.CheckBoundaries` gates EVERY boundary test, goals included, behind
> `z < Ball.Diameter` = 0.22 m, so a ball crossing the line airborne is neither a goal nor out of play);
> and there are **no blocked shots** (`BallCollisionHandler.OnAgentCollision` is called in production and
> its body is an empty `TODO`; posts are non-physical). In football ~30% of shots are blocked and ~30%
> miss the target — here both are approximately zero, a larger multiplier on the goal rate than anything
> a keeper does. **A4a stays blocked, but the reason is now specific: the shot-outcome distribution.**

> **Last Updated (prior):** July 26, 2026, later same day (v2.41 — **Foul & discipline balance pass landed (§5.Z.9)
> — the engine no longer empties itself of players.** Measured over one match-equivalent of composed play:
> **480 → 21.0 fouls, 147 → 3.0 yellows, 75 → 1.0 reds per 90 minutes** (football ~22 / ~3.5 / ~0.25), with
> no team dropping below eleven where the pre-fix engine reduced teams to five to seven inside nine minutes.
> **The measurement refuted the finding's own diagnosis:** §5.Z.7 framed this as a `[GT]` threshold question,
> but the peak qualifying-force distribution is bounded and narrow (p99 = 1175 N, **max 2362 N** — a
> collision impulse over `ContactDurationS` cannot exceed it), so the production gate replayed across a
> threshold ladder gives 480 fouls at 1200 N, 90 at 2000 N and **0 at 3000 N**. The threshold is a cliff,
> not a dial. What was missing is the referee: the model called *every* hard cross-team from-behind contact
> a foul, and the engine produces **seventeen per second**. `MatchEngine.cs` v1.48: `ApplyFoulIfCaptured`
> now computes `ComputeFoulCallProbability(F) = min(1, FoulCallProbability × F / FoulImpactForceThresholdN)`
> and partitions the **single** existing `match-flow.card-severity` draw — `u ≥ p` waves on (no event, no
> card, no restart, **no cooldown**, KD-F3), `u < p` whistles and takes the severity from the rescaled
> remainder `v = u / p` — so there is **no new RNG stream and no `SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_VERSION` change**;
> `MatchFlowCollisionConsumer` keeps the **strongest** contact of a tick rather than the first (KD-F4 —
> force now decides the call, so first-wins would systematically under-call the hardest fouls);
> new `_foulCandidateForceN` joins the existing always-reset-within-the-tick candidate triple (not
> serialized). `MatchEngineConstants.cs` v1.26: new `[GT] FoulCallProbability` = 0.015,
> `YellowCardProbability` 0.35 → 0.16, `RedCardProbability` 0.05 → 0.011, `FoulCooldownTicks` 60 → 180.
> **Calibration needed a LIVE run, not the offline sweep** — the sweep pointed at 0.025, which measured
> 37.5 fouls per 90 min, because giving 20× fewer fouls means 20× fewer restarts, so play runs on and the
> qualifying-contact count *rose* from 36 000 to 129 000. New `tests/MatchEngineDisciplineScenarios.cs` +
> `...Tests.cs` — the acceptance scenario `match-engine-discipline-plausible` (Tier B, 6 seeds × 9 min,
> ~52 s): rate bands, **no team reduced below nine players** (per seed, never aggregated), cards a minority
> of fouls; **9 of its 10 predicates fail on the pre-fix engine**, each by more than an order of magnitude.
> `MatchEngineFoulCardTests` v1.2 (+8: probability shape incl. fail-closed on a corrupt force, the wave-on
> leaving no trace and not leaking forward, strongest-wins capture driven through the real consumer with
> synthetic collision events). New env-gated `tests/FoulRateDiagnosticTests.cs` — the instrument, which
> replays the gate offline across a (threshold, cooldown, probability) ladder so ONE composed run yields
> the whole curve (the `[GT]` constants are `static readonly`, so an in-process sweep of the real gate is
> impossible) — plus `MatchEngine.TestOnly_SetCollisionObserver`, the seam that made the force distribution

> the WHOLE round (FR-SN-012), routing the managed club's fixture through a real `MatchEngine` and the rest
> through the model, applying every result to the table in FR-SN-013's pinned order, emitting one
> match-outcome per fixture, and advancing the cursor. `RoundResolutionModel` is **keyed, not
> cursor-positioned** (§3.4.1): `FixtureKey(seasonSeed, seasonNumber, roundIndex, home, away)` folds in
> `DOMAIN_TAG_SEASON_LOOP` — **the tag's first draw site, discharging ERR-030-001** — and feeds an exp-shaped
> lambda pair through a named **inverse-CDF** Poisson quantile (one uniform per side, `MAX_GOALS_PER_SIDE`
> cap), so permuting a round's resolution order yields the byte-identical table. Roadmap C1's whole point:
> a 20-club / 38-round / **380-fixture season resolves in milliseconds** instead of the ≥ 16 hours the real
> engine would need. New `tests/SeasonLoopTests.cs` (day-advance exactness, the FR-SN-026 byte-identical
> no-fixture-day floor against a bare `WorldStore`, per-club round completeness, order-independence, F4/F5/F6
> gates, mid-sequence restore through a real file, two-run determinism, the 380-fixture season) +
> `RoundResolutionModelTests` + the `season-multi-fixture` **capstone** on the #19 ScenarioRunner (season-save
> 179 → 240 tests; the capstone runs one real ~3.6-min engine match, the deliberate Simulation-layer
> home for that cost). **Full dotnet gate: PASSED, 0 failures (whole tree green; SDK 8.0.129 via apt).**
> **Three ERRs, and the third is the important one.** **ERR-030-012 / ERR-030-013**: two §4 architecture
> sketches that another section of the same spec forbids — §4.5's registered cursor stream (§3.4.1 requires
> keyed draws, so `SubsystemOrdinals.SeasonLoop` is deliberately NOT allocated: an ordinal with no stream is
> the FR-LW-031 phantom) and §4.6's "records the `MatchResult` in `SeasonState`" (§2.2/Appendix B have no
> outcome collection; the producer record is loop-scoped). **ERR-030-014, from running A4a's KD-8 Step 0
> pilot: a production match cannot develop play at all.** All 20 pilot matches finished 0–0 at a measured
> squad-rating gap of ±6; characterisation over 60 000 ticks found the ball's velocity **identically zero for
> the whole match**, never airborne, and **never possessed** — in both a distinct-squad and a plain neutral
> configuration. Closed loop, half of it already in the engine's own comment: `InitializeKickoffState` leaves
> the ball at rest (*"a kick would set it in motion; none at Stage 0"*), `RunFirstTouch` gate 3 will only grant
> a touch on a *moving* ball, production possession comes only from that path (`TestOnly_SetPossessor` is "Not
> called by production"), and only a possessing agent can kick. **A production match has always been a
> 90-minute 0–0 deadlock** — invisible because the 321 match-engine tests each drive their own inputs and the
> one composed test, the 600-tick kickoff capstone, asserts tick count / stride cadence / finiteness / bounds /
> digest advance, every one of which holds for a match in which nothing happens. **It verified that the
> composition runs, never that it plays.** Consequences: A4a is blocked upstream of itself (not by its compute
> — measured at ~98 s/match); the three round-resolution `[GT]` parameters ship **provisional, explicitly not
> fitted**; PM-1 is blocked, PM-2-sim is not. Owner is `match-engine-design.md` (new **§5.Z Phase H**), not
> #30; roadmap item **A4b** now precedes A4a. Committed with it: the A4a harness + fitter
> (`tools/round-resolution-fit.py`) + the env-gated Step 0 and diagnostic drivers + the evidence record
> `docs/tracking/round-resolution-corpus.md`. Self-review over the landing found 2 M — no gate enforced "the
> world is ON the round's fixture day" (a client could skip the day-advance for a whole career and get a
> plausible-looking table stamped with wrong world days), and the `FullEngine` routing branch was reachable
> only by running two real matches, so a typo in it would have shipped as "FullEngine quietly behaves like
> ManagedThroughEngine" (extracted to the pure `ShouldPlayThroughEngine`, all six combinations locked) — plus
> 3 L. See the path-to-playable OPEN ISSUES entry.)

> see both blobs, which is how **FR-LW-003** is honoured (neither `match-engine` nor `living-world`
> references the other; the root sits above, like `match-viewer` over `match-engine`). Files:
> `SeasonSaveConstants` (`[FIXED] SEASON_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION = 1` — a FOURTH format version, distinct from
> the two snapshot schema versions + `MATCH_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` + `WORLD_STORE_FORMAT_VERSION`),
> `SeasonSaveBlobs` (deframe result — two opaque byte sub-blobs), `SeasonSaveCodec` (pure static:
> `byte[] Encode(worldBlob, matchBlobOrNull)` / `SeasonSaveBlobs Decode(byte[])` — a version-gated frame
> around a `matchPresent` flag + two length-prefixed opaque sub-blobs, each keeping its own version gate;
> overflow-safe `Require` bound + fail-loud on bad format version / bad flag / out-of-bounds length /
> trailing bytes — the `MatchSaveCodec` posture), `SeasonSaveContents` (reconstructed `WorldStore` +
> nullable `MatchEngine`), `SeasonSaveManager` (static: `Save(world, matchOrNull, path)` = capture both →
> `Encode` → atomic temp→fsync→rename; `Load(path, ISquadProvider squads = null) → SeasonSaveContents` =
> read → `Decode` → `WorldStore.Restore` +, when present, `MatchSaveManager.Restore`). **KD-2:** the file
> is a thin frame over two SELF-CONTAINED blobs — `WorldStore.Snapshot()` (always) + the match save blob
> (only when a match is in progress, a `matchPresent` flag byte, KD-3) — the codec never parses either's
> internals, so all four inner versions are untouched (no `SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_VERSION` /
> `WORLD_STORE_FORMAT_VERSION` / `MATCH_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` change; the season file only adds the fourth
> frame version). **KD-5:** `MatchSaveManager` gains public `Encode(engine) → byte[]` +
> `Restore(blob, squads) → MatchEngine` (the "match save as a value" blob API — the capture seams stay
> internal); `Save`/`Load` refactored to delegate (behaviour-identical — all 279 match-engine tests still
> green). **KD-6:** the `ISquadProvider` is a Load-time parameter, never persisted, threaded into the
> match restore only when a match blob is present; the match restore's fingerprint + MXCSR float-mode
> gates run on season `Load` unchanged. New `season-save/tests/SeasonSaveManagerTests` (19): disk
> round-trip determinism for a no-match season (world field-identical via re-`Snapshot()` byte-equality +
> `world.text` resumes) and a season with a neutral / distinct-squad match (match digest chain
> byte-identical AND world field-identical, both through one file — the distinct-squad case exercises the
> provider threading through season `Load`), `SeasonSaveCodec` round-trip (with/without match, empty
> world) + all fail-loud gates, and `SeasonSaveManager` missing/corrupt/no-provider/null-world/overwrite.
> The booking-cursor (KD-8) case is a nested-match-blob property already locked by `MatchSaveManagerTests`
> (needs a match-test-internal seam), not re-driven at the season level — the season frame provably cannot
> alter it (KD-2). **Full dotnet gate run locally (SDK via apt): PASSED, 0 failures (whole tree green; 18
> new season-save tests). This closes snapshot-deserialize Phase 3 / the unified season save.** A fresh
> code adversarial-review pass over the shipped diff (0H+0M+2L, both fixed): L-1 restored
> `MatchSaveManager.Save`'s engine-before-path argument-guard order (the `Encode` delegation had flipped
> it — kept exactly behaviour-identical, `MatchSaveManager.cs` v1.2); L-2 added
> `Load_NoMatchSeason_WithProvider_IgnoresProvider` (locks R4, 18 → 19 tests). Gate re-run PASSED.)

> **Last Updated (prior):** July 17, 2026 (v2.22 — **Third repeat adversarial review (AR-3 of the July-16 cycle):
> 1 M, fixed.** M-1: `ApplyFoulIfCaptured` never checked `_isSentOff` on either foul participant —
> the foul/card/restart interpretation is itself a participation surface, and sent-off agents
> remain collision bodies (the physics forced-stop decelerates them, then they stand frozen in the
> path of play), so a red-carded agent repeatedly "won" free kicks (`ApplyRestart` teleported the
> ball to their feet) and drew cards against opponents who ran into their back, for the rest of
> the match. Fixed at the application site (`MatchEngine.cs` v1.35 — timing-equivalent to the
> capture site since capture and application share a tick and cards are issued only there; covers
> the `TestOnly_InjectFoulCandidate` seam; a single gate avoids the PM-AR-7-M-1 sibling-drift
> class); no event/cooldown/restart on a discarded candidate; physical collision response
> unchanged. +2 locks (`MatchEngineFoulCardTests` v1.1 — sent-off victim in the exact positive
> free-kick geometry, and sent-off offender). Verified clean this pass: every card path clears
> possession via `ApplyRestart` (no sent-off-possessor deadlock vector), the Interception case
> maps the Stage-0 unresolved interceptor to NO_POSSESSION, the gate-4 tie-break matches its
> doc, and all six AR-7/AR-8 fixes hold. Full dotnet gate re-run: PASSED, 0 failures.)

> captures at most one foul candidate/tick against a new `match-flow.card-severity` RNG stream;
> `_yellowCards`/`_isSentOff` discipline state feeds a Physics-phase forced-stop + `IsActive = false`
> in all four Mechanics-AI snapshot fill sites (#12/#13/#14/#15); `EvaluateAndApplyOffside` hooked
> into `RunFirstTouch`'s Controlled case; public `SubstitutePlayer` (bench swap, cap-enforced,
> queued `SubstitutionEvent` publish — the queue exists because `SubstitutePlayer` may be called
> between ticks when `EventBus.CurrentPhase` is not a valid producer phase); `CheckMatchFlowTransitions`
> (every Input phase) fires half-time (ball reset only — no ends-swap; `team 0 attacks +X` is
> hardcoded across goal detection/offside/Mechanics-AI, so a real ends-swap is a documented Stage-1+
> deferral) and full-time (`_matchEnded` freezes AI/Physics/Resolve) transitions, each once.
> **`SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 14 → 15** (discipline + substitution + match-flow-clock state, all
> cross-tick and digest-load-bearing). New tests: `MatchEngineRestartTests` / `MatchEngineOffsideTests`
> / `MatchEngineFoulCardTests` / `MatchEngineSubstitutionTests` / `MatchEngineMatchFlowTests` (pure-
> function locks + integration + two-run determinism each); `MatchEngineSnapshotSchemaTests` v1.12
> (pin 15 + 2 new probes). Per the user's process instructions, the design doc and the code each went
> through repeated adversarial-review rounds until a full pass found nothing new — the design-review
> cycle caught (among other things) that a full half-time ends-swap would break goal/offside
> detection for the second half, and the code-review cycle caught an `OffsideEvaluator` bug where
> fewer than two active defenders left the accumulator at `+Infinity`/`-Infinity` instead of `NaN`,
> making `IsOffside` return true for every finite attacker position (backwards from the intended
> rule) — fixed via an explicit active-defender count gating the `NaN` return. Full dotnet gate not
> runnable in this environment (no SDK access); verified by exhaustive manual review of the entire
> touched surface (not just the diff) in place of `dotnet test`.)

Survivors, each for a reason — the **shadow** (under any tilt a lofted ball separates from the pitch point it is over, the one cue perspective cannot supply), the corner→centre re-origin (it is the ground plane), and `FollowBallCamera` (it decides *where* the camera looks). Two things recorded rather than left implicit: the engine's Y becomes the world's **Z** and its Z the world's **Y** — an axis swap, the same trap class as the corner origin, locked by its own test (seven tests fail if it is inverted) — and `FollowBallCamera`'s pitch clamp is now **approximate**, since it describes a rectangle of visible ground where a tilted view sees a trapezoid; kept deliberately, as its job is keeping the target near the pitch rather than exact framing. What stays on the loop is `NextSeasonCalendar()`: the choice of the `[GT]` close season, bound once and read by both `AdvanceDays` and `RollToNextSeason`. New tests: `LiveMatchStreamerTests.cs`, `LiveMatchServerTests.cs` (real-loopback-socket routing), plus small additions to `MatchEngineGoalTests.cs`/`MatchEngineMatchFlowTests.cs` locking the new public properties. Full dotnet gate not runnable in this environment (no SDK reachable — `apt-get install dotnet-sdk-8.0`/`10.0` both 404 against the mirror) — verified by exhaustive manual review in place of `dotnet test`. |

| 2.8 | 2026-07-04 | — | Cross-spec **Mechanics chain** scenario corpus on the #19 `ScenarioRunner` (KD-8; owned by #19) — the first cross-spec chain beyond the physics-tier `{1,5}` `lofted-pass-kick-bounce-roll`, and the first that chains two **Mechanics-tier** orchestrators. New `src/testing-strategy/Tests/CrossSpecMechanicsScenarios.cs` + `CrossSpecMechanicsScenarioTests.cs` (owning specs **{12, 13}**, Tier B; paths under `SCENARIO_PATH_CROSS_SPEC_PREFIX`): drive the real `PositioningAITick` (#12) 10 Hz orchestrator live and read its per-tick formation-slot / line / phase output into a real `PressingAITick` (#13) snapshot each tick — the exact `FillPositioningSnapshot → Tick → FillPressingSnapshot → Tick` handoff `MatchEngine` performs. This is the composition surface the two per-spec suites each verify only their own half of: the #12 corpus ticks positioning against a static input, and the #13 corpus (v2.5) feeds pressing a **static** positioning state (never ticked, `SnapshotFactory` baseline slots). Here both real orchestrators run in sequence with #12's live output wired into #13's input each tick. **Scope caveat (adversarial-review finding):** the handoff is exercised *by construction* (the `Step()` slot/line copy into the pressing snapshot), NOT *asserted* by a predicate — the envelope checks below would still pass if that copy were removed and pressing read only its static baseline, and the determinism digest compares two identically-wired chains; the value is that the whole chain runs end-to-end deterministically, not a consumption proof. Two scenarios: `positioning-feeds-pressing` (20 ticks so the #12 phase-hysteresis + #13 debounce/role dwell advance; asserts the handoff carries real data + both halves stay well-formed — every own agent's live #12 slot is finite/non-sentinel/on-pitch, the #13 directive's `CoverShadowCount ∈ [0,2]`, `PrimaryPresserId ≥ −1`, every own pressing agent carries a defined `PressRole`) and `positioning-pressing-two-instance-determinism` (byte-identical FNV-1a-64 **composite** digest across two independent chains — #12 phase + per-agent lines AND the #13 directive + assignments; the pressing assignments are a downstream function of the #12 slots via `BaselineSlot`, so slot determinism is transitively locked without hashing raw float bits). Construction mirrors `MatchEngine` exactly: `new PositioningAITick(F442, 21)` → `SeedFromFormation(new PositioningPerceptionSnapshot(11))` (team-0 out of possession, opponent carries the ball ⇒ OOP shape) → `new PositioningAIView(pos)` → `new PressingAITick(view, new PassEventRing(8), 21)`, both snapshots carrying the monotonic tick index each F1 stale-guard requires. `testing-strategy-tests.asmdef` gains `PositioningAI` + `PressingAI` + `TacticalInstructions` refs. **No production change** — reads through the existing public orchestrator surfaces. Adversarial review: 0H+1M(scope, documented not code-fixed)+0L — M is the handoff-consumption caveat above (predicates check each half independently; the chain is run, not proven to consume — softened in the file-header SCOPE NOTE + scenario comment rather than papered over with a fragile `PrimaryPresserId >= 0` assertion the local env can't validate). Otherwise confirmed the `PositioningAITick.Tick`/`GetFormationSlot`/`GetLine`/`GetPhase` + `PressingAITick.Tick`/`LastDirective`/`GetAssignment` + `PressDirective`/`CoverShadow`/`PressAssignment`/`PressRole` member names against source; the F1 guard passes on tick 0 (`_lastProcessedTick` default 0, `0 < 0` false); struct-array element mutation (`snap.Agents[i].BaselineSlot = …`) is legal (array element is a variable, not a copy); the digest is float-free; and each chain builds fully independent snapshots + orchestrators (no cross-instance static mutation — the per-spec #13 determinism precedent). Files: CrossSpecMechanicsScenarios.cs (new), CrossSpecMechanicsScenarioTests.cs (new), testing-strategy-tests.asmdef. (dotnet gate not runnable in this environment — compile + suite verified in CI on push.) |
| 2.7 | 2026-07-03 | — | Goalkeeper Mechanics (#11) closed-loop scenario corpus on the #19 `ScenarioRunner` — extends the "orchestrator never executed" audit (Perception #7 v2.4 → Pressing #13 v2.5 → Attacking #15 v2.6, all Mechanics/AI-tier) to the last Physics-tier subsystem with a real 10 Hz + 60 Hz orchestrator. Audit of the 1,523-line `GoalkeeperMechanicsTests.cs` (11 fixtures) confirmed the gap: all coverage is pure sub-system functions (`GoalkeeperStateMachine` / reaction pipeline / dive kinematics / handling quality / cross-claim duel / rush dispatch / distribution) — `GoalkeeperMechanics` is instantiated nowhere and `TacticalTick` (10 Hz) / `Update` (60 Hz) are called nowhere. Unlike the Mechanics orchestrators, #11 is constructor-injected (`IGoalkeeperBallSystem` + `IGoalkeeperRngService`) with **no public state accessor**, so the corpus observes it *through its own injected dependencies* — the FR-CS-051..054 design point that makes it testable — via two recording test doubles (RNG draw log + ball-system call log). New `src/goalkeeper-mechanics/Tests/GoalkeeperScenarios.cs` + `GoalkeeperScenarioTests.cs` (owning spec {11}, Tier B) drive the real orchestrator over a **committed save whose dive misses a far ball**: GK0 (team 0, near its own goal) is carried Resting→Set→Anticipate by ball position + the Stage-0 anticipation stub, a `SaveIntent` is committed once it reaches Anticipate, and the orchestrator carries it Anticipate→Diving→Airborne, launching the dive. The ball is parked ~73 m away (deep in GK0's attacking third, x=75 ≥ `BallAttackingThirdXM`≈70) so the dive envelope can never reach it — the save **misses**, so the whole run is **publish-free** (no contact ⇒ no `SaveAttemptedEvent`/`BallClaimedEvent` ⇒ **no EventBus boot required**). Two scenarios: `save-launch-executes-dive` (15 tactical ticks × 6 Updates; asserts the composition actually ran the save pipeline through the injected deps — exactly one §3.3.2 dive-timing-jitter Gaussian draw fired from the correct draw site `DrawSiteDiveTimingJitter` under `DomainTagGoalkeeper`, zero uniform draws, and a missed save mutated no ball state: 0 ApplyKick / 0 SetPossessor) and `two-instance-determinism` (byte-identical FNV-1a-64 digest of both injected logs across two independent orchestrators). Behavioural depth stays covered by the sub-system suite; this locks the *composition* (both entry points run end-to-end deterministically, RNG threaded through the correct draw site). `goalkeeper-mechanics-tests.asmdef` gains the `TacticalDirector.TestingStrategy` ref (BallPhysics/AgentMovement already present). **No production change** — observation is purely through the injected interfaces. Platform-pin tokens `Stage0CertPlatformPin` (the cert tuple) + `LinuxNonCertPlatformPin`; `MatchEngineCapstoneTests.cs` v1.1 swaps its hardcoded non-cert pin string for the named constant (behaviour-neutral). | 1.61 | 2026-06-10 | — | First Touch (#4) AR-7 fix pass (1H+3M+3L) + AR-8 sweep (2M, both resolved; ERR-004-003..006 filed, ERR-004-005 documented-open). H-1/ERR-004-003: §3.3.2 direction-blend sign INVERTED — IncomingDir negated ball velocity (“direction the ball came FROM”) while the same spec subsection states four times that a q=0 touch continues ALONG its original path and §3.3.5 retains momentum along +v; a heavy touch teleported the ball up to 2.0 m back toward the passer while its velocity pointed forward (the ball then travelled back through the receiver). Spec §3.3.2 pseudocode patched in the same commit (section-3-1-to-3-5.md v1.4); OrientationDetector's negation is CORRECT (facing-vs-approach) and untouched. The test suite ENCODED BOTH conventions simultaneously (BD-002 travel-direction vs BD-003/BD-004 negated — mutually unsatisfiable) and has NEVER compiled: v1.1 closed the namespace before FirstTouchIntegrationTests leaving 170 '{' vs 171 '}'. M-1/ERR-004-004: INTERCEPTION proximity was agent-anchored via PressureRadius-truncated NearestOpponentDistance — §3.4.2 anchors at newBallPosition (anchor error up to 80% of the 2.5 m radius; opponents 2.5–3 m from the displaced ball but >3 m from the agent were invisible); PressureEvaluator now tracks the global nearest opponent + NearestOpponentPositionXY, PossessionStateMachine measures |opponent − newBallPos| ≤ radius (Stage 0 single-candidate approximation documented; full SpatialQuery + interceptor ID land with ERR-004-002). M-2: degenerate-blend fallback aligned to the §3.3.2 IncomingDir mandate (was agentFacing). M-3: §3.4.5 INTERCEPTION velocity redirect toward the interceptor implemented (was specified, never coded — Frame N+1 contact chain was broken). L: dead q/originalBallVel params dropped from Determine; ControlledThreshold doc no longer claims the CONTROLLED gate; dead PitchHalf* removed + ControlledRadius/DribbleDetachRadius doc-noted unconsumed; test side-comments. AR-8 sweep: M-1 EvaluateFirstTouch NaN gate — Mathf.Clamp01 does not filter NaN so NaN BallVelocity flowed into ControlQuality (EC-001 encoded the contract but could not pass); Step 0 sanitise maps non-finite velocity/direction components to 0 (parallels AM AR-10 / CS AR-7). M-2/ERR-004-006: §5.10 VS-001 hand-calc used an ADDITIVE below-reference velocity modifier (r=0.428 m) contradicting normative §3.2.3 (multiplicative excess-only → r=0.195 m); spec §5.10 v1.1 + §5 changelog v1.4 + test re-derived. ERR-004-005 (documented-open): the DEFLECTION alignment gate is effectively vacuous through the public pipeline — heavy-touch q makes retention dominate so alignment ≈ 1.0; PO-005's low-alignment LOOSE_BALL expectation was unreachable and is re-derived to lock actual behaviour. All expectations re-verified by a full-pipeline numerical mirror (21 scenarios). | 1.53 | 2026-06-08 | — | Pass Mechanics (#5) AR-8 fix pass: 0M + 3L on a fresh-eyes full-surface sweep over all 24 files in `src/pass-mechanics/` after the AR-7 changes landed. The other 7 AR-7-gated emits MUST keep the body-gate form because their if-bodies contain functional `_lastResult` + `return` follow-up, but this one is a pure-diagnostic if — in production the if-body became empty (`if (cond) { }`). Gate hoisted to wrap the entire if-statement: `#if … if (cond) Debug.LogWarning(…); #endif`. L-2: `PassExecutor.ExecuteContact` state transition (`_state = PassExecutionState.FollowThrough`) hoisted above Step 8 `EventBusStub.Publish(new PassAttemptEvent { … })`. The previous order was Step 6 `ApplyKick` → Step 7 populate `_lastResult` → Step 8 `Publish` → state transition. If `Publish` throws (queue overflow / registry mismatch), the ball is already kicked (Step 6) and the executor stays in `Contact` — next frame's `Update` re-enters `ExecuteContact` and calls `ApplyKick` a second time. The FM-08 possession recheck at line 358 currently saves the double-kick because the first kick released possession, but defensive ordering removes the dependence on that recovery seam: state is now committed before Publish runs, so a Publish exception leaves the executor in a recoverable `FollowThrough` state. Explanatory comment added at the new transition site naming the AR-8 L-2 invariant. L-3: forward-reference notes inserted next to the AR-2 M-2 history rows in both `PassExecutor.cs` v1.6 row and `PassTargetResolver.cs` v1.3 row. Those rows describe the AR-2-era contract as "[-1, +1]" — accurate at the time but superseded by AR-6 L-1 to "[-1, +1)" (24-bit mantissa quantisation never produces +1.0f). Historical rows preserved verbatim per the project convention of not rewriting history; forward-reference notes give readers chasing the bound through history a pointer to the current contract. | 1.51 | 2026-06-08 | — | Pass Mechanics (#5) AR-6 fix pass: 1M+3L all resolved, converts the AR-5 cycle-stop. M-1: input-mix primes for `frameNumber` / `passTypeIndex` in `PassErrorCalculator.ComputeErrorDirection` replaced with xxHash64 PRIME64_3 (`0x165667B19E3779F9`) and PRIME64_5 (`0x27D4EB2F165667C5`). AR-5 M-1 fixed agentId by spreading it through `0xC2B2AE3D27D4EB4F` (xxHash64 PRIME64_2) — explicitly NOT a Mix13 finalizer constant — but left the other two axes multiplying through `0xBF58476D1CE4E5B9` and `0x94D049BB133111EB`, the very Stafford Mix13 multipliers the finalizer then re-applies on lines 5 and 7 of the mixer. For adjacent (frame, passIdx) tuples at a fixed agentId, the prime reuse let the finalizer partially undo input-mix structure it was designed against uniform input. AR-6 closes the invariant on all three axes — input-mix primes are now disjoint from finalizer primes. L-1: `<returns>` and `<summary>` upper bound corrected `[-1, +1]` → `[-1, +1)`; the implementation produces `top24 < 2^24` so `normalised < 1.0f`, and EC-010 already enforces `Assert.Less(dir, 1.0f)`. L-2: inline comment block rewritten — stale "Each input is multiplied by a DISTINCT prime before XOR-folding" wording dropped (agentId is folded ADDITIVELY into the seed, not XOR-folded; the asymmetry is intentional AR-5 M-1), and the AR-6 input-mix / finalizer disjointness invariant recorded next to the prime annotations. L-3: bit-extraction literals `0x00FFFFFFu` and `0x01000000u` promoted to named local consts `Mantissa24Mask` and `Mantissa24Scale` with a comment noting the 24-bit window matches float mantissa precision (the divisor is exactly representable so the [0, 1) → [-1, +1) mapping introduces no rounding bias). Files: PassErrorCalculator.cs v1.7. Root CLAUDE.md AR-by-spec table row updated from `#5 AR-5 (1M+3L cycle-stop) ⚠` to `#5 AR-6 (1M+3L, converts AR-5 cycle-stop) ✓`. |
